www.lhratings.com  研究报告 1

内需亟待破局——10 月经济数据点评 
联合资信  主权部 
2026 年 10 月，宏观经济延续“外强内弱、新强旧弱”格局，出口韧性超预期构成核心支
撑，但内需偏弱矛盾进一步显性化。外贸端，10 月出口同比增长 35.2%，机电产品占比达 67.4%，
集成电路、自动数据处理设备等 AI 产业链产品高速增长，但集成电路等品类呈现“量减价升”，
价格因素对名义增速贡献显著上升；进口同比增长 41.6%，同样受 AI 产业链与能源价格推动。
投资端压力加剧，1—10 月固定资产投资同比下降 8.1%，房地产开发投资下降 21.6%，基建与
制造业投资同步走弱，仅知识产权产品投资（+9.2%）及高技术产业投资（+4.0%）维持韧性。
消费复苏动能衰减，10 月社零同比仅增 0.4%，服务零售额增速（4.9%）显著快于商品零售
（0.9%），汽车类消费构成主要拖累。价格端，CPI 同比上涨 0.7%、PPI 同比上涨 5.7%，剪刀
差扩大至 5 个百分点，反映输入性成本压力与内生需求偏弱的并存。总体看，新质生产力相关
领域仍是结构性亮点，但体量尚不足以完全对冲传统板块下行压力，内需修复仍需政策进一步
发力。

### Page 2

www.lhratings.com  研究报告 1
2026 年 10 月宏观经济运行延续了“外强内弱、新强旧弱”的核心格局，但内需
偏弱的矛盾进一步显性化，且价格端与需求端的分化呈现出新的特征。出口超预期韧
性仍是最大支撑，工业新动能持续高增，然而固定资产投资降幅加深、消费复苏动能
衰减、CPI 与 PPI 剪刀差走阔，共同指向经济修复的内生动力依然不足，政策托底的
必要性进一步上升。
一、进出口表现依旧强劲，价格因素贡献上升
2026 年 10 月出口表现强劲，增速虽较 9 月历史高位略有回落，但仍远超市场预
期，出口绝对额创历史同期新高。以美元计价，10 月出口 4123.3 亿美元，同比增长
35.2%，较上月微降 1.1 个百分点，已连续七个月保持两位数增长。2026 年 10 月进口
表现同样强劲，增速甚至超过出口，10 月进口 3070.5 亿美元，同比增长 41.6%，较 9
月（31.0%）大幅提升 10.6 个百分点。尽管出口额增速低于进口额，但出口数量增速
仍高于进口数量增速，价格因素对进口名义高增的贡献比出口更为突出，实际贸易顺

差增长好于名义表现。
AI 产业链是核心引擎，但价格因素贡献上升。出口高增的首要支撑来自全球 AI
相关产业的高景气。机电产品 10 月出口 2779.82 亿美元，占出口总额比重约 67.4%，
同比增长 46.1%，增速较上月进一步上行。其中，集成电路出口同比飙升 137.1%，自
动数据处理设备及其零部件同比增长 114.8%，两者是机电品类中增速最快的分项。
高新技术产品整体出口增长 66.6%，同样维持高位。
但值得注意的是，部分品类的高增更多由价格因素而非数量扩张驱动。以集成电
路为例，10 月其出口数量同比由正转负（从 0.9%降至-2.5%），而出口价格同比从 133.8%
进一步上行至 143.2%。这意味着 AI 相关商品的价格上涨对名义出口增速形成了显著
推升，实际出口量的增长力度可能弱于表观读数。
与此同时，传统劳动密集型产品出口增速明显回落。家具玩具、服装箱包等品类
10 月增速较上月走弱，出口增长的动力重新向高端制造集中，结构扩散效应有所减
弱。
AI 相关产品是进口增长的第一引擎。10 月自动数据处理设备及其零部件进口同

比增长 261%，集成电路进口增长 78.3%，两者合计拉动进口增长约 21.7 个百分点，
贡献了超过一半的进口同比增量。这与出口端集成电路、自动数据处理设备的高增形
成呼应，反映出全球 AI 产业链的贸易流在中国进出口两端同时放量。能源类商品同
样呈现“量减价升”特征。10 月原油进口金额同比增长约 41%，但进口数量同比下
降 13.3%；煤炭进口金额增长 60%，数量同比下降 5.5%。国际油价中枢抬升直接推
高了进口支出，而国内实物需求的恢复力度从这个数据看并不强劲。

### Page 3

www.lhratings.com  研究报告 2 
东盟稳居第一，对欧创新高，对美受低基数支撑。分贸易伙伴看，东盟仍是第一
大出口市场，10 月对东盟出口 779.75 亿美元，同比增长 46.3%，虽较 9 月的 52.3%
小幅放缓，但仍显著高于整体增速，占出口总额的 18.9%。基建需求、补库周期及产
业链转移带动的中间品贸易是核心拉动力。东盟仍是最大进口来源地，10 月自东盟
进口 461.0 亿美元，1—10 月累计增长 28.1%。自越南进口增长尤为突出（当月同比
约 55%），反映产业链转移背景下中间品贸易的深化。自美国进口当月约 137.1 亿美
元，累计增长 7.8%，增速相对温和。

二、固定资产投资格局深化，新动能是稳定增长项 
2026 年 1—10 月全国固定资产投资降幅进一步扩大，是拖累内需的主要因素。
国家统计局数据显示，1—10 月全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 8.1%，降幅
较前三季度（-7.2%）继续加深，且显著弱于市场预期（预期为-7%）。从当月同比看，
10 月当月固定资产投资下降 16.76%，显示投资动能仍在收缩。 
三大领域投资全面走弱格局进一步深化，新动能成为固定资产投资中稳定增长
项。房地产开发投资是最大拖累项，1—10 月同比下降 21.6%，降幅较前三季度进一
步扩大。新建商品房销售面积同比下降 13.7%，销售额下降 14.3%，销售端未见企稳
迹象。基础设施投资同比下降 5.1%，同样较前三季度的-4.2%走弱。尽管信息传输业
投资增长 32.0%、航空运输业增长 12.5%，但水利、公共设施管理等传统基建板块降
幅较大（水利管理业-16.7%，公共设施管理业-14.1%），对整体基建形成拖累。制造业
投资同比下降 2.9%，较前三季度（-2.1%）降幅扩大。 
分行业看，铁路船舶航空航天等运输设备制造业投资增长 14.1%，是少数亮点；
但汽车制造业（-7.9%）、专用设备制造业（-9.0%）、医药制造业（-9.2%）等板块收缩
明显，反映传统制造领域扩产意愿偏弱。在整体投资下行背景下，部分新兴领域投资

保持增长。知识产权产品投资同比增长 9.2%，是投资中少有的稳定增长项。高技术
产业投资同比增长 4.0%，其中信息服务业增长 20.5%、航空航天器及设备制造业增长
10.2%、电子及通信设备制造业增长 6.6%。设备工器具购置投资同比增长 8.9%，反映
大规模设备更新政策仍在发挥一定效果。 
分地区看，东北地区投资下降 25.9%，中部（-12.9%）、东部（-11.5%）、西部（-
9.2%）均呈两位数或接近两位数的降幅，区域分化中东北压力尤为突出。民间投资同
比下降 11.4%，降幅大于整体投资，扣除房地产后仍下降 7.8%，显示民营企业投资信
心依然不足。 
总体来看，10 月固定资产投资延续并加剧了此前的下行趋势，房地产深度调整、
基建实物工作量形成偏慢、制造业扩产意愿不足压力叠加。然而，新质生产力相关投
资持续发力，一定程度对冲传统投资下行压力，成为固定资产投资中唯一的稳定增长
项。

三、消费内部分化显著，服务消费强、商品消费偏弱 
2026 年 10 月消费数据表现疲弱，增速进一步回落。国家统计局数据显示，1—
10 月社会消费品零售总额累计同比增长 1.0%。10 月，社会消费品零售总额 46463 亿
元，同比仅增长 0.4%，较 9 月回落 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.02%。 
服务消费仍强于商品，汽车是主要拖累项。2026 年 1—10 月服务零售额同比增
长 4.9%，比商品零售额增速（0.9%）快 4 个百分点。旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲等服
务品类增速明显快于整体服务零售。10 月，商品零售同比仅增 0.3%，餐饮收入增长
0.7%，增速回落主要受中秋错月（去年中秋在 10 月、今年在 9 月，部分消费提前释
放）以及部分耐用消费品销售放缓影响。分行业看，除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比
增长 2.5%，而整体社零增速仅 0.4%，汽车类消费对整体消费形成了显著拖累。不过，

限额以上单位通讯器材类、文化办公用品类 10 月同比分别增长 6.5%和 4.1%，部分
升级类商品保持较快增长。从前 10 个月累计看，通讯器材类增长 16.5%，可穿戴智
能设备零售额增速超过 70%。 
总体来看，10 月消费延续了服务消费仍强于商品的分化格局，汽车拖累显著，政
策托底品类（通讯器材等）尚有支撑，但整体消费动能较前三季度进一步走弱，居民
预防性储蓄倾向和消费信心修复偏慢的问题未见明显改善。

四、上游价格高企、下游需求疲弱 
10 月 CPI 同比上涨 0.7%，涨幅较上月回落 0.2 个百分点；PPI 同比上涨 5.7%，
涨幅较上月扩大 0.7 个百分点。PPI 与 CPI 剪刀差扩大至 5 个百分点。 
从 CPI 端看，食品价格同比下降 2.9%是主要拖累，鲜菜、鸡蛋等价格大幅下跌
抵消了汽油价格上涨的影响。但更值得关注的是核心 CPI 同比仅上涨 0.8%，涨幅同
样回落 0.2 个百分点，表明剔除食品和能源后的内生性价格压力依然偏弱。从 PPI 端
看，涨幅扩大主要集中在上游能源相关行业，汽油价格同比上涨 22.4%影响 CPI 同比
上涨约 0.63 个百分点，地缘政治驱动的输入性成本压力是 PPI 上行的主因，而非国
内工业品需求的实质性改善。中下游企业面临原材料成本上升而终端售价难以同步传
导的困境，利润空间受到挤压，这在一定程度上解释了制造业投资意愿偏弱和传统工
业板块收缩的持续性。 
总体来看，10 月出口的韧性虽然超预期，但其对经济增长的净拉动在价格因素
和贸易条件变化下可能被高估，投资和消费的双重疲弱使内需成为稳增长的主要短板，
而政策工具的传导时滞意味着短期内的企稳仍需耐心。新质生产力的强劲扩张是当前
经济中最确定的结构性亮点，但其体量尚不足以完全对冲传统板块的下行压力。在外
部地缘风险与贸易博弈持续扰动的环境下，以自身发展的确定性对冲外部不确定性，
需要政策在扩大内需上展现出更强的力度和更精准的指向。

儒鴻(1476 TT) Trading Buy

營運穩定成長，長期展望正向。 
投資建議：預估儒鴻 2026 年營收為 408.71 億元，YoY+7.59%，
稅後 EPS 24.79 元。2027 年預期在產能稼動維持高檔及客戶訂
單穩定下，營收成長至 424.43 億元，YoY+3.84%，稅 後 EPS 25.52
元。考量：(1)3Q26 毛利率如預期的順利回升，預期未來幾個季
度的毛利率可維持現有水準。(2)持續投入自動化設備更新提高
生產效率，並擴建新產能因應客戶需求，印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投
產，推動長線的成長動能。綜上所述，對儒鴻的投資建議為
Trading Buy，目標價 385 元 (2027 年 PER 15.5X)。  
預估 4Q26 營收維持成長，稅後 EPS 6.09 元：儒鴻 3Q26 營收
維持高檔為 105.72 億元，YoY+9.74%， QoQ+4.82%，毛利率亦
受惠客戶新產品出貨增加，產品組合優化及匯率相對友善等因
素，順利提升至 31.18%，稅後 EPS 為 6.36 元，符合預期。預期
4Q26 由於成衣價格上漲且客戶新品訂單持續出貨，可推動儒鴻
營收維持成長，預估儒鴻 4Q26 營收為 105.93 億元，
YoY+14.34%，QoQ+0.20%，毛利率 31.23%，稅後 EPS 6.09 元。 
美國期中選舉結束，市場不確定因素消除，儒鴻訂單能見度維
持六個月左右：美國期中選舉在民主黨獲勝的結果下，可預期
極端的關稅政策不會再發生，不確定性因素逐漸消除，不過目
前也沒看到品牌端有追加訂單的動作出現，預期未來幾個月的
訂單及終端銷售會處於正常的水準。目前儒鴻的訂單能見度維
持在六個月左右，市場庫存也在相對健康水準，整體市況穩定。 
儒鴻持續投入自動化設備更新提高生產效率，並擴建新產能因
應客戶需求，印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投產：儒鴻持續擴增新產能及
自動化設備更新以因應客戶需求，除了在台灣、越南二地的生
產設備持續更新外，印尼廠新產能也計劃在 4Q27 產能投產。目
前印尼廠的產能利用率維持滿載水準，印尼廠的生產需求未來
的成長空間仍大，預期等印尼廠三期投產後，可為儒鴻帶來下
一段成長的動能。

### Page 2

個股報告

2 群益投顧 
  儒鴻的 ESG：  
儒鴻 2016 年即制定溫室氣體盤查管理程序，並依循「儒鴻企業環安衛及能
源政策」設置溫室氣體盤查推行委員會，由董事長室擔任主任委員，推動廠
區溫室氣體盤查，並以 2017 年作為公司溫室氣體盤查的基準年，推動溫室
氣體減量。此外，致力於用水回收，除了在製程蒸氣冷凝回收使用外，更引
進新技術改善廢水回收。 
公司簡介： 
儒鴻為台灣主要紡織業者，具染整、織布、成衣上下游一貫生產能力，主要
產品為各式成衣，並生產機能布料應用在各類運動休閒服飾上。國際各知名
服飾品牌及通路商如 NIKE、 UA、 LULULEMON 、 TARGET、 COSTCO 皆
為儒鴻的客戶。 
預估 4Q26 營收維持成長，稅後 EPS 6.09 元： 
儒鴻 3Q26 營收維持高檔為 105.72 億元，YoY+9.74%，QoQ+4.82%，毛利率
亦受惠客戶新產品出貨增加，產品組合優化及匯率相對友善等因素，順利提
升至 31.18%，稅後 EPS 為 6.36 元，符合預期。 
預期 4Q26 由於成衣價格上漲且客戶新品訂單持續出貨，可推動儒鴻營收維
持成長，預估儒鴻 4Q26 營收為 105.93 億元，YoY+14.34%， QoQ+0.20%，
毛利率 31.23%，稅後 EPS 6.09 元。 
美國期中選舉結束，市場不確定因素消除，儒鴻訂單能見度維持六個月左
右： 
美國期中選舉在民主黨獲勝的結果下，可預期極端的關稅政策不會再發生，
不確定性因素逐漸消除，不過目前也沒看到品牌端有追加訂單的動作出現，
預期未來幾個月的訂單及終端銷售會處於正常的水準。目前儒鴻的訂單能
見度維持在六個月左右，市場庫存也在相對健康水準，整體市況穩定。 
儒鴻持續投入自動化設備更新提高生產效率，並擴建新產能因應客戶需求，
印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投產： 
儒鴻持續擴增新產能及自動化設備更新以因應客戶需求，除了在台灣、越南
二地的生產設備持續更新外，印尼廠新產能也計劃在 4Q27 產能投產。目前
印尼廠的產能利用率維持滿載水準，印尼廠的生產需求未來的成長空間仍
大，預期等印尼廠三期投產後，可為儒鴻帶來下一段成長的動能。 
投資建議： 
預估儒鴻 2026 年營收為 408.71 億元，YoY+7.59%，稅後 EPS 24.79 元。2027
年預期在產能稼動維持高檔及客戶訂單穩定下，營收成長至 424.43 億元，
YoY+3.84%，稅後 EPS 25.52 元。考量：(1)3Q26 毛利率如預期的順利回升，
預期未來幾個季度的毛利率可維持現有水準。(2)持續投入自動化設備更新
提高生產效率，並擴建新產能因應客戶需求，印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投產，推
動長線的成長動能。綜上所述，對儒鴻的投資建議為 Trading Buy，目標價
385 元 (2027 年 PER 15.5X)。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。 
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌，亦將出具降評報告。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

全球经济温和复苏，价格带动进口增速反超出口
——2026 年 10 月进出口数据及全球经济点评
事件：2026年10月，我国进出口总额为7193.8亿美元，同比
增长 37.9%。其中，出口金额为 4123.3 亿美元，同比增长
35.2%；进口金额为 3070.5 亿美元，同比增长 41.6%；贸易顺
差为 1052.8 亿美元，同比增长 19.6%。
一、10月进出口数据解析
 10 月出口延续高位增长，海外制造业扩张和科技产业链需求

构成主要支撑。10 月出口同比增长 35.2%（以美元计价，下
同），较 9 月小幅放缓 1.1 个百分点，但仍明显高于 2021—
2025年10月约5.9%的平均增速。从外部环境看，10月全球制
造业PMI为52.6%，连续15个月处于扩张区间，全球制造业仍
处上行周期为短期商品贸易提供支撑。值得注意的是，本轮制
造业扩张更多由AI、国防等领域资本开支以及投资品和中间品
需求驱动，相关需求对价格和融资成本的敏感度相对较低，因
而在高利率和通胀压力下仍保持较强韧性。与此同时，能源及
物流扰动导致全球供应商交付周期延长、积压订单增加，部分
经济体面临产能和原材料约束，我国产业链完整、生产响应较
快和交付能力稳定的优势进一步显现，有利于承接全球订单。
此外，去年同期出口基数偏低，也放大了本月同比增幅。
 亚洲区域贸易保持活力，韩国和拉美市场成为本月较为突出
的增量来源。10 月我国对东盟出口同比增长 46.3%，占出口
总额的比重升至 18.9%，对整体出口增速拉动约 8.1 个百分
点，仍是出口增长的最大拉动项。 亚洲产业链内部出现一

市场多元化在对冲部分传统市场增速放缓方面发挥了积极作用。
 科技产品的主导作用进一步增强，算力设备拉动上升，汽车和传统商品贡献则有所
减弱。10 月机电产品出口同比增长 46.1%，占出口总额的 67.4%，对整体出口增速
拉动 28.7 个百分点，较9 月提高1.1 个百分点；高新技术产品出口增长 66.6%，拉
动出口增长 18.1 个百分点，较 9 月提高 0.7 个百分点。其中，集成电路出口增长

137.1%，拉动出口增长约 7.5 个百分点，仍是最主要的细分支撑；自动数据处理设
备及其零部件出口增长 114.8%，拉动作用由 9 月的 5.1 个百分点升至 6.1 个百分点，
成为本月科技产品中较为突出的增量来源。相比之下，汽车及底盘出口增速由
62.0%回落至 37.6%，拉动作用由 2.4 个百分点降至 1.8 个百分点；服装、纺织品、
玩具、家用电器、塑料制品和家具出口虽然均保持两位数增长，但合计拉动由 9 月
的约 3.8 个百分点降至2.8 个百分点。总体看，10 月出口高增更多依靠科技产业链
支撑，算力设备的拉动作用进一步增强，而汽车和传统商品贡献边际减弱，出口增
长动力较 9 月有所集中。

 价格因素推动进口增速大幅上升，科技产业链与能源商品共同推动进口增速超过出口。
10月进口同比增长41.6%，较9月加快10.6个百分点，创五年来新高。在出口仍保持
35%以上增长的情况下，进口进一步提速，推动当月贸易顺差由 9 月的 1335.3 亿美元
收窄至1052.8亿美元，减少282.5亿美元。值得注意的是，尽管今年以来进口增速持
续走高，但商品结构集中度也同步上升，且价格对进口总额的带动明显强于规模，能
源品与中间品进口的增多并不代表内需的改善。10 月进口增长动力明显向能源商品扩
展，呈现“科技产业链保持强劲、能源商品拉动跃升”的特征。不过，10 月国内制造
业 PMI 重新回落至收缩区间，铁矿石、农产品等与传统生产和消费需求关联较强的商
品进口仍较弱，内需尚未全面回暖。
 高技术产品仍是进口增长的基础性支撑，能源商品则贡献了本月进口加速的主要
边际增量。10 月高新技术产品进口同比增长 72.1%，对整体进口增速拉动 23.3 个

百分点，较9月提高约2.0个百分点。其中，集成电路进口增长78.3%，拉动进口
增长 13.7 个百分点，较9 月提高约0.8 个百分点；自动数据处理设备及其零部件
进口增长 261.0%，拉动约 8.0 个百分点，虽较 9 月略有减弱，但仍处于较高水平。
更值得关注的是，能源商品对进口增长的合计拉动由 9 月的约 1.3 个百分点大幅
升至 6.8 个百分点，是进口增速明显加快的主要原因。其中，原油进口金额由 9

海外经济分化加深下外贸韧性能维持多久？
——2026 年 8 月进出口数据点评
议升温，我国科技产品出口对全球科技投资周期的依赖进一步加深，而汽车及传统商品
增长动力有所减弱，出口增长的持续性面临一定考验。贸易政策方面，美国中期选举后
国内政策博弈可能加剧，现有关税安排仍具有阶段性，主要经济体针对重点产业的贸易
限制和供应链本地化趋势亦难以逆转。此外，中东局势反复及能源运输风险仍可能推高
生产和物流成本，进一步压缩企业利润空间。总体看，短期出口仍具较强韧性，但后续
能否保持较快增长，将更多取决于科技投资景气的持续性、传统商品需求恢复及外部贸
易环境的变化。
二、全球经济跟踪：全球经济保持温和增长，通胀黏性与政策不确定性加剧市场调整
近一个月，从宏观经济表现来看，美国经济增长加快，欧洲温和复苏，日本内需仍然偏
弱。通胀黏性与科技投资不确定性成为市场关注重点，一方面，继 9 月美联储加息后，11月
英国央行进一步加息控制通胀水平。另一方面，AI 投资回报担忧引发科技股明显调整，新兴

市场资本外流扩大。全球贸易受AI 产业链需求支撑保持韧性，但能源运输风险和地区分化仍
然突出。此外，11 月 3 日，美国中期选举民主党夺取国会两院控制权，特朗普政府施政面临
更多制约，也给外部环境带来新变局。总体看，全球经济尚具韧性，但增长动力集中、通胀
反弹及政策约束加大，经济金融运行的不确定性进一步上升。
 主要经济体增长分化，能源冲击推动生产端通胀加快上行，货币政策面临更大约
束。美国经济保持较强韧性，三季度 GDP 环比折年实际增长 3.4%，较二季度加快
1.2 个百分点，消费和投资共同支撑增长，其中 AI 相关资本开支持续扩张，但对
就业及居民收入的带动相对有限。9 月 CPI 同比上涨 3.7%，能源价格上涨加大通
胀压力。继 9 月加息 25 个基点后，美联储 10 月维持政策利率在 3.75%—4.00%，
两名委员主张进一步加息，反映通胀风险仍受到高度关注。11 月 3 日中期选举中，
民主党赢得参众两院多数席位，选民对高生活成本和中东战争的不满集中显现。
共和党失去国会控制权后，特朗普政府推动新增财政刺激、税收和其他立法议程

的难度上升，但关税及对外政策仍具有较强行政裁量空间，后续政策博弈可能加
剧经济不确定性。欧洲经济温和修复，日本内需仍显疲弱，通胀压力使得欧、日
央行货币政策维持偏紧。三季度欧元区 GDP 环比增长 0.4%，同比增长1.3%，德国
小幅回升，法国表现偏弱。10 月欧元区通胀升至 4.0%，其中能源价格同比上涨
20.8%，加剧居民购买力和企业成本压力。日本9月核心CPI同比上涨2.1%，居民

消费恢复缓慢，日本央行 10 月维持政策利率 1.25%不变。总体看，中东能源冲击
使主要央行面临更突出的增长与通胀权衡，货币政策由此前的宽松预期转向维持
限制性立场甚至进一步紧缩，尤其加大了能源进口依赖型经济体的滞胀压力。
 全球金融市场波动加剧，AI 投资回报担忧引发科技股调整，能源价格与利率预期
变化推动资产重新定价。近一个月，金融市场呈现明显的结构性分化。10 月中旬
至今，标普 500 指数下跌约 3.3%，纳斯达克指数下跌约 7.7%，道琼斯指数则上涨
约 1.4%，反映资金由高估值科技股向价值和防御性板块轮动。尽管美国企业盈利
总体保持韧性，但市场对 AI 资本开支持续扩张、债务融资增加及投资回报兑现的
担忧升温，美国中期选举后数据中心建设政策的不确定性进一步加剧市场调整。
债券市场方面，避险需求上升推动美国 10 年期国债收益率由 5.2%左右降至当前
的 4.86%。大宗商品方面，中东局势反复推动油价先涨后跌，布伦特原油 10 月上

涨约13%，11月初随着地缘冲突缓和预期升温明显回落，当前降至约92美元/桶；
黄金亦呈现较大波动。总体看，近期全球资产价格调整是高利率环境下 AI 投资回
报、能源供应风险及政策不确定性共同推动的结构性重新定价。
 全球金融条件偏紧与科技股调整加剧新兴市场资本外流，但我国跨境资金仍保持
净流入。IIF 数据显示，10 月新兴市场证券投资净流出 338 亿美元，较 9 月修订
后的 193 亿美元进一步扩大，为 3 月以来最大单月流出规模，与上年同期净流入
274 亿美元形成鲜明对比。亚洲科技股调整、美元相对强势及主要经济体利率维
持高位，共同加大新兴市场资本流出压力。相比之下，我国跨境资金流动总体稳
健。国家外汇管理局数据显示，9月银行代客涉外收付款顺差407亿美元，连续六
个月保持净流入，但较8月的624亿美元下降约35%；同期银行结售汇顺差562亿
美元，反映境内外汇供求总体平衡、资金流入仍具支撑。结构上，货物贸易项下
资金保持较大规模净流入，但企业对外直接投资及境外关联企业融资有所增加。

总体看，外部金融环境收紧对我国跨境资金流动形成一定扰动，但贸易顺差及相
对稳定的市场预期仍提供支撑，需关注后续资本流入放缓及汇率波动风险。
 中东冲突仍在持续博弈拉锯中，能源运输和全球供应链仍面临较大不确定性。10
月以来，美伊冲突持续围绕霍尔木兹海峡及周边能源设施展开，11 月初多艘商船
遇袭，但卡塔尔等国也重启斡旋，局势出现一定缓和迹象。与此同时，也门政府

～前期比年率▲0.4％の小幅マイナス成長を予想～ 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト 新家義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

11 月16 日に公表される26 年７-９月期の実質
ＧＤＰ成長率を前期比年率▲0.4％（前期比▲
0.1％）と予測する。10 月 30 日の段階では前期
比年率＋0.4％（前期比＋0.1％）と小幅プラス
成長を予想していたが、その後公表された経済
指標の結果を反映し、小幅マイナス成長へと予
測値を下方修正する。 
本日公表された26 年９月分の国際収支統計の
結果を反映し、26 年７-９月期の実質輸出を前期
比＋0.1％（従来予測値：同＋1.2％）へ下方修
正する。筆者の９月分の想定と比べてサービス
輸出が大きく下振れたことが主因である。実質
輸入も前期比＋3.0％（従来予測値：同＋
3.3％）と下方修正するが、輸出の下方修正度合
いの方が大きいことから、外需の予測値を前期
比年率寄与度▲1.9％Pt（従来予測値：同▲
1.3％Pt）へ下方修正している。 
また、９月分の家計調査と家計消費状況調査
が想定よりも下振れたことを反映し、個人消費
の予測値も前期比▲0.3％と、従来予測値の同▲
0.2％から小幅下方修正した。

物価高による下押しや耐久財需要の反動に加え、天候不順の影響もあって個人消費が減少する
ことが成長率を押し下げる見込みである。特に、８～９月には長雨・大雨や台風などの天候不順
が相次ぎ、外出手控えや旅行・宿泊の見送りなどを通じて消費が下押しされたとみられる。一
方、設備投資は高い伸びとなり、景気を下支えするとみられる。設備投資の伸びには特殊要因も

のマイナス成長には天候不順という一時的な下押し要因も含まれており、過去３四半期に高い成

流れが途切れたと判断する必要はないだろう。
物価高による節約志向は根強く、個人消費の回復にはなお時間を要するとみられるが、設備投
資を中心に企業部門が好調さを維持している点は好材料である。家計部門が力強さを欠く一方、
企業部門が景気を下支えする構図のもと、日本経済は均してみれば緩やかな回復基調にあると判
断している。

### Page 1

ab 10 November 2026
Global Research
First Read
ASMedia Technology Inc
Inventory adjustment weighs on N-T outlook
Q326 earnings broadly in line; PC demand remains weak
ASMedia reported Q326 EPS of NT$26.39, broadly in line with UBSe but above Street,
helped by ~NT$1.5bn of non-op income mainly from WT Micro. Q326 sales of NT
$2.66bn declined 2% QoQ and 33% YoY, reflecting continued weakness in PC demand
amid elevated memory prices and the shift at its major customer from AM5 back to the

lower-cost AM4 platform. Q326 GM came in at 51.6% (+1.3ppt QoQ), helped by a
more favorable product mix and pricing adjustments.  Opex increased to NT$795mn on
higher R&D spending including 12nm chip tape-out and a 10-15% increase in
headcount expansion, resulting in  OpM declining 2.1ppt QoQ to 21.7%.  Q326 sales mix
was 74% for host controllers, ~21% automotive and ~5%  device.
Sell-through improving, but  inventory adjustment to weigh through Q127F

ASMedia is seeing improving PC motherboard demand, with Q326 motherboard
production up >20% QoQ and management viewing H126 as the near-term trough.
However, elevated memory prices have driven AMD's product mix back toward the
lower-ASP AM4 platform (AM4  >70% of Q326 chipset shipments up from >50% in
Q226), while AMD continues to digest inventory. It noted AMD's sales contribution has
already declined from >50% of sales in H126 to <50% in Q326, and management

expects the inventory correction to continue through Q127, delaying the benefit from
improving motherboard sell-through. We expect October to mark the trough (monthly
sales down 43% MoM) and will remain muted on AMD's inventory control, resulting  in
Q426 sales  declining~33% QoQ  while GM should decline by 1.6ppt QoQ to 50.0% as
slower pull in of AM5 could lead to inventory provision, despite a reversal in H127E.
2027E recovery more back-end-loaded

ASMedia remains confident in retaining its sole-supplier position for AMD’s next-
generation Olympic (AM5.5) chipset platform, with contribution expected from H227
and ASP ~50-60% higher than AM4. We expect USB4 demand to recover alongside the
Olympic ramp, with additional upside from a new US robotics customer from H227.
Separately, we expect AM5 shipments to recover  and potential  inventory writebacks to
provide upside to GM. We believe PCIe packet-switch contribution could be more

gradual given slower design-in progress. Overall, we estimate ASMedia’s 2027E sales to
grow ~5% YoY (vs. previous 17% YoY expectations), with growth skewed to H227.
Valuation: Retain Buy with PT of NT$1,500
We lower our 2026/27/28E EPS by 2%/1%/2% primarily on softer PC demand and
inventory adjustment, offset by higher better earnings contribution from WT Micro. We
cut our PT to NT$1,500 (from NT$1,700), based on our SOTP valuation  (18x for core

earnings and 11.5x for non-core), implying 13x 2027E PE.  Maintain Buy rating as stock is
trading near trough 14x NTM PE  vs an 11-48x historical range, while its diversification
into non-PC   should support its topline/OP to recover from 2027E.

Company Description
ASMedia is a Taiwan-based fabless IC design house founded by parent company Asustek
Computer Inc. in 2004. ASMedia is dedicated to high-speed input/output (I/O) controllers,
such as USB, PCIe and SATA, and its products are mainly used in desktop PCs, motherboards

and add-on cards. In 2024, ASMedia generated 84% of its revenue from host controllers and
16% from device controllers. The company collaborates with Advanced Micro Devices (AMD)
to design southbridge chipsets for Zen-based desktop central processing units (CPUs). AMD is
the company's largest client account, contributing 58% of revenue in 2024.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We base our price target on sum-of-the-parts methodology. We value ASMedia's core and

non-core businesses based on a target PE multiple.
Fabless integrated circuit (IC) design houses face a variety of uncertainty, including rapidly
changing technology, end-market demand, competition, employee turnover and foundry
wafer costs. They are also vulnerable to legal disputes over intellectual property rights.
We believe risks for ASMedia include: 1) AMD's chipset insourcing or other design houses
entering AMD's chipset business; 2) faster penetration of USB4 native support in chipsets; 3)

more entrants in the USB device controller market; 4) continued suspension of China tender
projects; and 5) increased US export controls impacting China's computing capability.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over the next 12
months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is the one-year local
market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR) Stocks may be flagged
by the analyst to indicate that the stock's price target and/or rating may change in the near term, usually in response to an event that

may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have a 12-month investment horizon.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We update our view after 3Q26 results (issued 6 November).

What’s the impact: Fundamentals largely on track. Rising CPU demand 
from agentic AI has lifted our 2026 regular server growth target to >50% 
YoY (vs. 20-30% YoY), making regular servers the key growth driver for 
Wiwynn and helping it offset the slower-than-expected AMZN ASIC AI 
server ramp in 3Q26 (ramping from September vs. original July estimate). 
Looking ahead, we expect the T3 lifecycle to likely peak in 2Q/3Q27, but 
the rising contribution from both Nvidia and AMD GPU AI servers should 
offset the slowdown in 2H27. As for the margin trend, we expect regular 
servers to be at 10% and ASIC AI servers to be at 8%. However, we expect 
the rising GPU AI server to gradually dilute the blended gross margin as 
Wiwynn focuses only on L11, while GPU motherboard (L6) and tray level 
(L10) will be handled by its parent company Wistron (3231 TT, TWD171, 
Buy [1]). As such, we expect the gross margin for Wiwynn’s GPU AI servers 
to be 2-3%, lower than the 4-5% AI of other ODM peers.

3Q26 results beat expectations. Wiwynn reported 3Q26 net income of 
TWD23.36bn (EPS: TWD41.46), beating our expectation by 8%. 3Q26 
sales (TWD434.62bn; up 56% QoQ and 63% YoY) beat our estimate by 6% 
and the Bloomberg consensus estimate by 7% on stronger-than-expected 
regular server shipments. Regular servers accounted for 50-55% of its 
revenue, down from 60-70% in 2Q26, while AI servers contributed 45-50%. 
Gross margin declined by 0.4pp QoQ to 8.9%, but still exceeded our 8.2% 
estimate. Management attributed the sequential margin contraction mainly 
to lower NRE recognition and rising material costs, as not all memory 
components used in regular servers are procured under consignment 
arrangements. However, we believe lower regular server contribution to the 
product mix was key reason. With better operating leverage, operating 
margin improved by 0.2pp QoQ to 7.5%. That said, net profit was dragged 
down by higher interest expense (reaching TWD3bn in 3Q26 vs. TWD2bn 
in 2Q26) and a higher tax rate of 23.2% (vs. 21.1% in 2Q26).

What we recommend: We lift our 2026-27E EPS by 1-5% to reflect 
stronger regular server demand but cut 2028E EPS by 3% to reflect higher 
interest expense assumption. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating but trim our 
12M TP to TWD2,500 (from TWD2,715), based on a target PER of 13x 
(prior: 18x; within its past-2-year trading range of 9-21x) applied to our 1-
year forward diluted EPS, given weaker market sentiment. Key downside 
risks: weaker-than-expected server demand and CSP capex.

How we differ: Our 2028E EPS is 3% above street estimates due to our 
higher regular and AI server shipment assumptions.

11 November 2026

3Q26 review: fundamentals largely on track

➢ Regular server strength sustaining into 2H26, thanks to agentic AI 
➢ Increasing AI server projects from 4Q26 to boost revenue momentum 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; trimming 12-month TP to TWD2,500

Company profile 
Established in 2012 and listed in 2019, Wiwynn (a subsidiary of Wistron Corp) is a dedicated ODM-
direct supplier of servers for hyperscale datacentres, with major customers including Facebook, 
Microsoft, and Amazon. Aside from hyperscaler customers, the company is expanding its target markets 
to what it terms "Next Wave" customers, including tier-2 cloud service providers and telecom operators, 
which could bring growth opportunities related to edge computing and open RAN.

### Page 5

5

Wiwynn Corp (6669 TT): 11 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 11 Nov 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Wiwynn plans to have seven to nine directors on its board who will serve three-year terms in
accordance with the Articles of Incorporation in Taiwan. Currently, Wiwynn has 9 board
members with five independent directors. The background of independent directors is diversified
and their competence includes technology, business management, and law. We deem the
diversified background as a positive factor for the company’s board quality. Wiwynn held 9 board
meetings in 2025 and the average attendance rate was around 99%.
The Chairman does not serve concurrently as the President or CEO, which we think is a positive
for board and executive management as well as external shareholder interest.
Capital management 2
Wiwynn declared cash DPS of TWD145 and a stock dividend of TWD20 per share for 2025,
representing a total payout ratio of 60.0%. We note that investors favour its high dividend payout
ratio policy. However, Wiwynn has been expanding its own manufacturing capacity in Malaysia,
Tainan, Mexico and the US, making its business model less asset-light than before. With the
server market still in the high growth period and the payout ratio remaining at around 60%, we
expect the favourable dividend policy to be sustained going forward.
Related party &
transaction 3
In 2025, revenue from related parties accounted for 0.2% of total revenue, while purchases from
related parties were 9.2% of cost of sales. The main purchase counterparty is Wistron (3231 TT;
Wiwynn's parent company), with purchases from Wistron accounting for 8.09% of total
procurement. While Wiwynn is a professional assembly vendor for data center servers, it still
outsources a portion of its production capacity to its parent company, Wistron. At the same time,
Wiwynn has expanded its own production bases in Malaysia, Tainan, Mexico and the US,
reducing its reliance on Wistron. This business model still causes meaningful transactions with
related parties, although the exposure has declined materially. Indeed, we are concerned
whether Wistron collects fair manufacturing fees from Wiwynn. As the company states that
trading terms of purchase transactions with related parties cannot be compared with third-party
vendors due to product specifications, we are not able to assess this concern.
S Supply chain management 1
Wiwynn focuses on providing various products and system solutions for hyperscale datacentres
and cloud infrastructures. There are over 100 suppliers providing key components, electronic
materials, and mechanical materials in Wiwynn’s supply chain. For the new joiners that target to
penetrate into Wiwynn’s supply chain, Wiwynn has formed the “Supplier Evaluation Group” to
conduct: 1) supplier management and supply capabilities evaluation, 2) technical capability
development assessment, and 3) partner quality assurance (these include mainly topics
undertaken by RBA, such as no child labour, no conflict minerals, no use of environmentally
hazardous substances, etc.). New suppliers are also required to be ISO9001, ISO14001, IECQ
QC 080000, and OHSAS 18001 (ISO 45001) qualified.
Wiwynn has strong supply chain risk management by conducting audits regularly. Apart from
considering the technical skills, quality date, delivery dates and price completeness of suppliers,
Wiwynn audits its suppliers to endeavour to protect the environment, ensure safety and health of
the environment, and emphasise the importance of human rights, based on Wiwynn’s CSR
Management System.
S Data security 2
To serve world-class cloud service providers, Wiwynn has been constantly improving its
information security management. Employees are educated on the protection of the product,
technology, and information privacy of customers.
In response to the global trends in data protection laws that tend to strengthen the security of
classified information, Wiwynn has mandated that all assigned PCs have the Windows Bitlocker
HDD encryption activated to avoid classified data being accessed without permission. In 2020, it
also introduced Multi-Factor Authentication (MFA). Beyond the security measures for hardware,
the company controls access to office, labs, and conference room in a robust manner to prevent
theft of the hardware.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

Aspeed (5274.TWO): Mgmt. visit: BMC and SMC specification migration 
and customers’ expansion to drive growth ahead; Buy
16 November 2026 | 11:05PM HKT
We visited Aspeed in Taipei recently. Management remain positive on future growth, 
riding on further customer expansion, product mix upgrade, and their continuous 
new product launches. The company highlights its comprehensive coverage across 
various chipset platforms (e.g. GPU, ASIC, RISC-V, etc.) and coverage across different 
layers in AI data center (e.g. component-level, server-level, rack-level, data 
center-level), securing their leading market position. We remain positive on Aspeed 
and see the growing servers demand along with AI servers design getting more 
complicated to support the company’s growth ahead. Maintain Buy. Read more: 
Aspeed Oct revenues and 3Q26 results in line. 
Key takeaways 
1. Positive tone on future growth: Management remain positive on future business 
growth, driven by further customer expansion, product mix upgrade, and their 
continuous new product launches. The company’s shipment is mainly from BMC2600 
in 2026, and 1700 and 2700 would further expand shipment in 2027, carrying higher 
ASP as stronger specification (e.g. integrated more functions, I/O supporting DDR5). 
The company would also have 2705 prior to 2800, which offers stronger security. The 
2800 is targeted to start sampling in 2028, and would be a driver beyond 2028, 
integrating more functions, stronger security (e.g. silicon-level security), stronger I/O, 
and using more advanced processing nodes. On SMC and Platform, the company will 
also have new products entering mass production in 2H27: (1) SMC 1040 and 1840 
(embedded FPGA, serving both single-node and multi-node), and (2) platform 1080, 
aiming to further expand customers. The company’s pure exposure to the server 
market also brings it strong end market demand, with the ability to pass through the 
higher raw material costs to end customers (e.g. higher costs from substrate, OSAT, 
foundry), supporting its GM. 
2. Competition advantages: Management highlight they are early in the market and 
serve various customers (e.g. GPU, ASIC, RISC-V, etc.), with comprehensive product 
coverage across different SoC platforms, and the company is also expanding its 
product portfolio vertically: across SMC (component-level mgmt.), BMC 
(server-level), and RMC (rack-level). The company’s products can monitor various 
scenarios, such as GPU thermal issue, liquid cooling leakage, etc. Management also 
highlight their large R&D team (over 100 engineers), local and timely support (having 
R&D team in Taiwan, close to customers), and ecosystem (work closely with ODMs). 
We remain positive on Aspeed’s leading market position and see AI server 
specifications getting more and more complicated (e.g. more shelves, more chips per

### Page 2

rack, from air cooling to liquid cooling, etc.), which would further enhance Aspeed’s 
competition edges. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Aspeed (5274.TWO) 
Valuation: Our 12-month TP of NT$22,000 is based on a target P/E of 40x (in line with 
its 10-year historical trading average) applied to our 2028E EPS, and discounted back to 
2027E at 13.6% CoE. Our key assumptions for CoE include: (1) 1.5x beta (sourcing 
Bloomberg), (2) a 4.25% risk-free rate, and (3) a market risk premium at 6.25% (in line 
with GS house view). 
Key downside risks to our view and TP: 1) A softer-than-expected server market 
demand recovery, 2) slower-than-expected BMC penetration within AI servers, 3) 
intensifying market competition.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 16 Nov 2026 close.

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적 
2026-10-29

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 640,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 285,500 원 
상승여력: 124.2%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 감소 및 환율 하락으로 시장 예상치 하회 
Key: 4Q26~1Q27 부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 수주 모멘텀 전망 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 엔진&FDC 으로 밸류 매력도 충분. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 시장예상치 하회하는 실적 시현  
3Q26 연결 매출액 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영업이익 8,992억원
(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(9,792억원)를 하회했다. 조업일수 감소
(휴가 10 일+중대재해 4일) 및 환율 하락 영향(450 억원)과 더불어, 임단협 관련 예상 비
용 중 75%를 이번 분기 선반영 했기 때문이다. 상선 수주 빈티지는 중형 선박 기준 ‘23 년 
9.6%, ‘24년 65%, ‘25년 25%, 대형 선박 기준 ‘22년 4%, ‘23년 45%, ‘24년 31%, 
‘25년 20%로, 선가 상승효과 지속되는 중이다. 특히 해양 부문에서 루야 프로젝트의 공정
률 확대 효과 및 C/O로 180억원의 환입 발생으로 전 분기 대비 큰 폭의 이익 성장 시현했
다. 최근 키윗과 미국 내 합작 조선소 설립을 추진하며, 미 군수지원함 초도 2척 수주 및 향
후 물량 건조, 그리고 HII와의 함정 선체블록 하도급 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 64 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 82 만원에서 64 만원
으로 하향한다. 최근 상선 실적 피크아웃 우려에 따른 조선주 주가 급락으로 멀티플 하향 조
정했다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 
FDC 에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지속되고 있는 중이다. 올해 공시 기
준으로 육상발전 엔진 약 1.6GW 수주한 것으로 알려졌으나, 미공시된 약 1.4GW 까지 포
함 시 총 3.0GW 이상을 수주한 상황이다. 캐파 증설 기준으로 ‘28 년 1.5GW, ‘29 년 
3GW 캐파에 해당하는 물량을 이미 대부분 채웠으며, 기존 계획 대비 빠른 준공 혹은 공정
률 확대, 더 나아가 추가적인 캐파 증설까지 고려할 수준으로 파악된다. 빠르면 올해 연말 
혹은 내년 초부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 본격 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 
전망이다. 현 주가 레벨에서 상승여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 3Q 실적 및 임단협 불
확성 해소된 현 시점에서 적극적 매수 의견 및 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)로 제시한다.

본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다. 
투자판단 3 단계(6 개월기준) 15%이상 → 매수 / -15%~15% → 중립 / -15%미만 → 매도

Document 292: Anthropic IPO需求火爆：多笔“百亿级别天量订单”现身，还有巨亏的币圈资金

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic IPO需求火爆：多笔“百亿级别天量订单”现身，还有巨亏的币圈资金.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic IPO 热度持续升温， Hyperliquid 平台上与其估值挂钩的合约未平仓头⼨已超
3.34 亿美元，参与者中不乏加密⽼⼿。币安联合创始⼈赵⻓鹏及多位市场⼈⼠将加密市场
近期资⾦外流部分归因于此。与此同时，稳定币供应量 30 天内缩⽔约 165 亿美元，⽐特币
约半数筹码处于亏损。
摘要：
Anthropic 即将上市正在成为市场最炙⼿可热的交易主题。 11 ⽉ 17 ⽇，据彭博报道，据知情⼈⼠
透露，Anthropic 的 IPO 已收到多份⾦额超过 100 亿美元的认购订单。他们表⽰，预计银⾏于美
东时间 17 ⽇下午 4 点停⽌接受认购订单。
与此同时，加密货币市场资⾦持续外流，部分资⾦被认为已转道押注这家 AI 公司的 IPO 。在
Hyperliquid 平台上，与 Anthropic 估值挂钩的两份合约未平仓头⼨已累计超过 3.34 亿美元。参与
这些加密挂钩 Anthropic 押注的交易者中，此前有⼤量⼈曾活跃于主流加密期货市场。
币安联合创始⼈赵⻓鹏（ CZ ） 11 ⽉ 15 ⽇在 X 平台发⽂，将⽐特币近期下跌的潜在原因之⼀指

向 " 资⾦向 Anthropic IPO 转移 " 。他写道： " 没有⼈能确切知道加密价格为何下跌，相关性并不等
于因果关系，但这是⼀个可能的原因。 "
加密借贷机构 Ledn 联合创始⼈ Mauricio Di Bartolomeo 表⽰： " 如果你看⼀看更具投机性的投资
机会，我认为⽬前 AI 投资的脉搏跳动得⽐ DeFi 更有⼒。 "
稳定币⼤幅缩⽔，链上亏损创近⽉新⾼
资⾦离场的迹象在稳定币市场尤为明显。
据彭博援引 Glassnode 数据，截⾄ 11 ⽉ 15 ⽇的 30 天内，稳定币总供应量缩⽔约 165 亿美元，降幅
主要来⾃ Tether 的 USDT 和 Circle 的 USDC 。 Glassnode 指出，这两种代币余额的下滑集中在交易
所端，意味着交易场所内可⽤于交易的资本正在减少。
⽐特币价格⽅⾯，近⽇持续在约 64,000 美元附近或以下交易，较 9 ⽉底约 86,600 美元的收盘价已
下跌逾 25% 。 Glassnode 数据显⽰，⽬前约半数流通中的⽐特币，其持有成本⾼于当前市价 ——

⽽ 9 ⽉底时这⼀⽐例不⾜四分之⼀。
周⽇链上数据同样不乐观： Glassnode 估算，当⽇⽐特币链上亏损⾦额是盈利⾦额的 5.6 倍，为 7
⽉中旬以来最⼤差距。
AI 叙事抢占散⼾注意⼒
Glassnode ⾸席分析师 Frederik Theissen 在报告中写道： " 整体数据显⽰，过去⼏周资本正在迅
速离开加密⽣态系统， 8 ⽉份⼊场的投资者正在抛售。总体⽽⾔，市场对加密和⽐特币的热情⽬
前并不⾼涨，这在⽐特币熊市筑底阶段是典型现象。 "
AI 股票正在提供曾经吸引散⼾进⼊加密市场的诸多要素：⼤幅波动、关于颠覆性技术的宏⼤叙
事，以及⼀夜暴富的想象空间。但与加密资产不同，科技公司股票背后是真实的商业模式 ——
投资者可以审视营收增速，追问巨额⽀出何时能够回本。
美国现货⽐特币 ETF 上周净流出也超过 8.5 亿美元，进⼀步印证了资⾦撤离的趋势。

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

写评论
图⽚
此轮下跌并⾮单⼀因素所致。
据彭博报道，美国国债收益率⾛⾼、美元⾛强构成宏观层⾯的压⼒；近期 Binance 安全漏洞事件
重燃市场对交易所安全性的担忧；美国中期选举⺠主党获益，也令市场对加密⾏业监管前景增
添不确定性。
Orbit Markets 联合创始⼈ Caroline Mauron 表⽰： " 该资产类别对整体市场情绪依然敏感。
Anthropic 若能成功上市，将带来正向动能；但若上市过程出现波折，则会迅速波及包括⽐特币
在内的所有⻛险资产。 "

Economics | 3

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• We did not materially alter our real PCE forecast, and expect consumer spending growth to
continue.
Consumer spending has held up remarkably well this year despite higher gasoline costs weighing on
household purchasing power. While we do not expect the recent above-trend near-4% annualized
pace of real consumption growth to be sustained, we see consumers continuing to spend at a healthy

clip into next year. A firmer income backdrop revealed by recent revisions, continued support from
household wealth and an expected recovery in purchasing power as inflation slows should keep
consumption on solid footing. We expect real consumption growth to remain north of 2% in the final
quarter of the year and roughly match this year’s average pace in 2027.
Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products, and Inventories
• A stronger-than-expected capex outturn in Q3 has revealed a slightly firmer trend headed into

next year.
Business investment continues to be driven by the high-tech buildout, with real equipment spending
stronger than expected in Q3 and the latest data suggest a firmer trend. We have not materially
changed our outlook following the Democratic sweep in the midterms. Our forecast already assumes
some moderation in high-tech investment next year and a smaller contribution to GDP growth as the
buildout matures. Beyond high-tech, we expect only a modest broadening in capex spending over the

coming year.
Source: U.S. Department of Commerce and Wells Fargo Economics
Source: U.S. Department of Commerce and Wells Fargo Economics
Investment: Residential
• We have not made major changes to our outlook for residential investment. High mortgage rates
and challenging affordability conditions look set to continue pressuring home sales and new single-
family construction.
Not much has changed in the way of residential investment. Mortgage rates have come in somewhat,

but are still elevated above 7.0%. Building permits are stuck in a low gear, with single-family projects
continuing to moderate against a backdrop of weak buyer demand and elevated inventories.
Multifamily permits have picked up modestly, reflecting renewed strength in apartment demand.
Modestly lower mortgage rates and stabilizing apartment market conditions should help put a floor
beneath residential investment and bring about modest growth over the course of next year.

Investment: Nonresidential Structures
• Structures investment is likely to maintain healthy momentum amid robust data center, power, and
related nonresidential construction.
4 | Economics

### Page 5

Economic Outlook: November 2026 Economics
Structures investment accelerated notably in Q3. The bulk of this momentum can be traced to data
centers and power, however, activity also appears to be broadening out. As examples, private spending
on communication and transportation infrastructure have perked up and manufacturing construction
appears to be stabilizing on increased semiconductor investment. The downturn in commercial
construction has also softened as data centers shore up demand for warehousing. Although new

regulations may soften the pace of data center starts, there are more than enough projects already in
the pipeline to support healthy structures investment through next year.

Net Exports & Trade
• Net exports should remain a fairly persistent drag on U.S. real GDP growth amid continued
imports.
A surge in imports, particularly of high-tech goods like computers, caused net exports to subtract

more than 3 percentage points from Q3 GDP growth. We expect trade to remain a drag in Q4, though
with little data in hand, that largely reflects where trade flows ended last quarter and an assumption
of another sizable gain in imports. Looking ahead, continued strength in high-tech investment should
keep import growth elevated and the trade deficit wide, leaving net exports a persistent drag on U.S.
growth. Exports remain more volatile, but imports are increasingly the bigger driver of the trade story.

Labor Market
• Labor demand has improved modestly in recent months. We now expect payroll growth to average
65K per month in 2027, and the unemployment rate to hold steady at 4.1% over the next four
quarters.
The October jobs report reinforced our view that the labor market remains solid. The pickup in job
growth this year aligns with purchasing manager surveys that point to expanding employment across
the goods and services sectors. At the same time, the historically low trends in initial jobless claims and

the layoff rate suggest employers broadly remain reluctant to shed workers. Structurally, firms appear
focused on retaining existing staff and investing heavily in AI adoption while remaining selective about
adding to head count—a dynamic that should keep the low-hire, low-fire environment largely intact.
We expect payroll growth to average around 65K per month in 2027, roughly in line with our estimate
of breakeven job growth. That should keep the unemployment rate steady at 4.1% through next year

and labor market conditions generally consistent with full employment.
Inflation
• We look for the core PCE deflator to rise 3.3% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis in 2026 before slowing to
2.6% in 2027.
The October inflation report reinforced the persistence of price pressures, with upside surprises
emerging across a range of categories. Although gasoline and diesel prices have receded in recent
Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
weeks, earlier increases in transportation costs are still working their way through supply chains. Food
inflation picked up in October and looks poised to strengthen further. The outlook for energy prices
remains highly uncertain and represents the largest swing factor for inflation, in our view. Within the
core, we continue to expect goods inflation to moderate in 2027 as tariff-related pressures fade
further and demand tied to the AI buildout cools. Services inflation, however, is likely to prove stickier.

Health care services prices have been running hot and should continue to face upward pressure from
the aging population, and primary shelter inflation looks to be stabilizing. Taken together, we expect
inflation to remain above the Fed's 2% target through next year and look for the core PCE deflator to
end 2027 at 2.6% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis.

Fiscal Policy
• The lame duck session of Congress probably will not yield a budget reconciliation bill or a debt

ceiling resolution. The December 11 government funding deadline probably will be punted to Q1/
early Q2 via another continuing resolution.
• We project a federal budget deficit of $2.0 trillion for FY 2027, similar to FY 2026 in dollar terms
but a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).
Assuming that Congress does not increase or suspend the debt ceiling in the next six weeks, the
incoming Democratic Congress will need to negotiate a resolution with President Trump in 2027.

Right now, it looks like Treasury will hit the debt ceiling around early March. At that point, Treasury can
lean on extraordinary measures and its cash on hand to last for about 7-8 months, putting the debt
ceiling "X date" somewhere around early Q4. We suspect the debt ceiling/annual budget negotiations
will include a lift to defense and nondefense discretionary government spending for FY 2027/2028,
but we doubt the bump will be enough to meaningfully move our economic growth or deficit forecasts.

For now, our working assumption is that divided government will not produce any major new fiscal
expansions/contractions over the next two years, leaving the budget deficit picture as status quo until
after the 2028 election.
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• Our baseline forecast is for another 25 bp rate hike to the fed funds rate in December, followed by
an extended hold from the FOMC through 2027. The risks are tilted toward 1-2 more hikes early

next year.
• Our forecast for the 10-year Treasury yield at year-end 2027 is 4.85%.
The recent inflation data seem to make a hike in December a lock so long as financial conditions don't
completely implode in the coming weeks. The core CPI came in about 8 bps above consensus at 0.3%
for October, and a 0.4% on core PCE is plausible, depending on how the PPI data shake out. This should
put the FOMC's hawks back in the driver's seat after the October skip.

6 | Economics

### Page 7

Economic Outlook: November 2026 Economics
Past December, the outlook will be highly dependent on future inflation data. Our expectation is that
core inflation will cool gradually in 2027, and we still do not see clear signs of demand-side inflation in
the data. But hiking cycles have an inertial component to them, and the recent inflation data reset the
clock to zero on the Fed seeing several good inflation prints in a row. This creates some modest upside

risk to our base case for the fed funds rate next year.

International Developments
• Global growth has proven more resilient, with our 2026 growth forecast revised higher and a
modest improvement in the underlying 2027 outlook.
• Our 2026 inflation forecast has moved slightly lower, while our 2027 forecast has moved higher,
with inflationary risks likely to persist into 2027.
• The monetary policy bias remains toward extended tightening across advanced economies.

Emerging market policy paths remain differentiated, but have also shifted in a more hawkish
direction, with some central banks bringing forward rate hikes and others having less room to ease
in 2027.
The balance of risks to global inflation remains tilted to the upside. Elevated geopolitical uncertainty,
resilient activity and persistent energy and supply-chain pressures increase the risk that inflation
remains higher for longer. Although the direct effects of supply shocks may prove temporary, central

banks are increasingly focused on delayed pass-through, persistent services inflation and potential
second-round effects on wages, prices and inflation expectations. Higher government bond yields are
meanwhile keeping financial conditions tight. The result is a shift toward more extended tightening,
with several rate hikes moving further into the forecast horizon and less room for easing in 2027.
Across advanced economies, the tightening cycle is increasingly extending into 2027. Our European

Central Bank outlook is unchanged, with a 25 bps hike in December still our base case, although
tighter financial conditions raise the risk of a later move. In the U.K., our November Bank of England
hike was realized, and we now expect another 25 bps hike in Q1-2027, reflecting renewed inflation
pressures associated with the Ofgem price-cap reset.
Elsewhere, the policy outlook has turned more hawkish in Canada. Having previously flagged upside
risks to our year-end call, we now expect the Bank of Canada to hike at consecutive meetings, bringing

the policy rate to 3.00% by Q1-2027. In Australia, the Reserve Bank of Australia raised the Cash Rate
again in November. After five hikes, we think policymakers have room to assess how tighter policy
feeds through the economy, although risks remain skewed toward another increase if inflation stays
elevated or activity proves stronger than expected. Japan is unchanged: we continue to expect the
Bank of Japan to hike in December and again in 2027, bringing the terminal rate to 1.75%.

Emerging market policy paths remain differentiated but have also shifted more hawkishly. In India,
resilient growth and rising inflation have led us to bring forward our second Reserve Bank of India
Economics | 7

### Page 8

Monthly Economics
hike from Q1-2027 to December 2026. Brazil is moving toward higher-for-longer policy, with
persistent inflation raising the bar for additional easing; we now expect cuts to resume in Q2 rather
than Q1-2027 and see less easing overall next year. In Mexico, Banxico's cut to 6.25% was more easing
than we expected, and with inflation risks still tilted to the upside, we now expect rates to remain on
hold. China remains the exception: continued targeted policy support and resilient exports should

support growth, prompting most upward revisions to our 2026 and 2027 growth forecasts, while
greater scope for easing likely emerges in 2027.

A poor start to the week for tech stocks has caused some
dovish repricing in the USD curve, but the dollar has retained its
good momentum. We still feel the AI story has the best
potential to hit the dollar at this stage. Nvidia’s earnings will
drive market sentiment today. EUR/USD may hold up around

Francesco Pesole, Chris Turner and Frantisek Taborsky
Nvidia enters a new chapter without Jensen Huang at the helm

USD: Staying strong
The drop in US tech stocks has done little to dent the dollar’s strong
momentum. That is probably because the sell-oﬀ has spilled over
suﬃciently into broader US and global equities for the dollar to

Today’s main focus is Nvidia’s quarterly earnings, with the
company’s long-term prospects under increased market scrutiny
following CEO Jensen Huang’s departure. Speculation that he might
be reinstated was dismissed yesterday. Nvidia’s results are always
a key test for the AI trade, but even more so in the current
environment. We still think further weakness in tech/AI stocks

should make additional greenback appreciation less sustainable as
it drives more dovish Fed repricing.
On the data front, October US retail sales are due today.
Expectations are for a slowdown from September, but still slightly
positive MoM growth: 0.1% in the headline and 0.3% ex auto and
gas. October industrial production is also expected at 0.1% MoM.
These aren't tier-one releases, and equity market dynamics still
have a greater chance of driving FX moves today.

There is also more Fedspeak, after Kevin Warsh refrained from
monetary policy comments yesterday and the hawk Beth
Hammack left the door open for outsized rate hikes. We’ll hear
again from Chris Waller, alongside Musalem (hawkish leaning) and
Williams (dovish leaning).
We are sticking to our view of some dollar stabilisation rather than
another major leg higher. Still, the macro nature of the dollar’s
bullish momentum means corrections may be more easily faded.

GBP: Slightly softer jobs ﬁgures not
impactful
UK jobs ﬁgures released this morning were weaker than expected,
but unlikely to have much impact on Bank of England tightening

plans. October payrolls surprisingly declined by 34k, but the
September ﬁgure was revised higher from 10k to 20k. September
unemployment was also a tad lower than expected at 4.6%. The
closely monitored wage ﬁgures were also a bit softer in September,
with weekly earnings ex-bonus down from 3.7% to 3.5% in
September.
Expect much greater market sensitivity around tomorrow’s CPI
report for October. The consensus is for a small acceleration from
3.5% to 3.6% in the headline.

Pricing remains aggressive on the Bank of England, with a
December hike having an 80% implied probability and 90bp of
tightening in the next 12 months. Our baseline is for the next move
only in February, but we admit the risks are of an earlier hike.
EUR/GBP continues to face some upside risks, but the 0.83-0.84
range may dominate as a range into the new year.

NOK: Caught between an oil correction
and higher rates
The Norwegian krone has been the worst-performing currency in
the G10 since the start of November, losing 4.7% vs USD and 3.3%

vs EUR. The correction is primarily due to the sharp drop in oil
prices, but also mirrors an unwelcoming environment of hawkish
Fed repricing and risk sentiment instability for the least liquid
currency in the G10.
The very attractive carry should protect the krone once global risk
sentiment ﬁnds some more sustainable stabilisation. But for now
downside risks persist, especially as Norges Bank has signalled
greater caution in further tightening, removing some short-term

protection for the currency.
Our EUR/NOK target for year-end is 10.65, but risks are skewed
towards exploration above 10.70 -10.75 in the coming weeks.
Anyway, NOK continues to face greater downside vs USD.

Document 366: India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26)

Source PDF: all report pdf/India_ A middle class too small to supplant China’s economic role (2026-10-26).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 8
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
At a time when Chinese consumption is slowing significantly, could India become a driver of global consumption? For now, this
prospect presents considerable difficulties, with many obstacles to overcome. Despite dynamic growth and the Indian economy’s
expanding share of global output (8.2% of worldwide GDP in 2025), the middle-class consumer population remains modest and is nowhere
near the size of China’s.
This is due to the concentration of employment in low-value-added and largely informal sectors. The few manufacturing sectors with

high value-added and strong job potential — electrical equipment, electronics, automotive, chemicals and pharmaceuticals — continue to
face constraints from an unfavourable business environment and a skills shortage that is holding back their move into premium market
segments.
Furthermore, the highest-paying service sector jobs, particularly in information technology, are now threatened by the rise of
artificial intelligence. Without concrete measures, the “demographic window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to

25 years — could close before India establishes a solid industrial base and a sufficiently large middle class.
INDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
Johanna Melka
issue 20
26 October 2026

### Page 2

2
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
worldINDIA: A MIDDLE CLASS TOO SMALL TO SUPPLANT CHINA’S ECONOMIC ROLE
INDIA IS STRUGGLING TO ESTABLISH A ROBUST MIDDLE
CLASS
With an annual growth rate of 7.3% over the past decade (excluding the
pandemic), India has one of the highest growth rates among the world’s
major economies. Yet, its middle-class consumer base remains surpri-
singly small. When measured in current U.S. dollars, total consumption

is very high in India (USD 2,241 billion in 2025), but this figure prima -
rily reflects the country’s overall population size. By comparison, this
consumption level falls 3.5 times short of China’s corresponding figure.
Furthermore, per capita consumption adjusted for purchasing power
parity (PPP) is 1.3 times lower than that of Indonesia, the Philippines
and Vietnam. When evaluated at constant prices, consumption per ca-
pita has increased only slightly over the past thirty years compared to

China and even Vietnam (Chart 1).
According to our estimates, based on the research conducted by Ló -
pez-Calva and Ortiz-Juarez (2014) 1, India’s middle-class consumer
segment accounts for just 1.6% of the total population (see Table 1),
or fewer than 24 million people. This is less than in Thailand and Viet-
nam (more than 30 million each) and far below China (approximately
490 million, according to the same definition).
The majority of households identified as middle class by standard In -

dian research institutions (the Centre for Monitoring Indian Economy
[CMIE] and the National Council of Applied Economic Research [NCAER])
would, in fact, be categorised as “vulnerable” according to World Bank
criteria: households that have officially emerged from poverty yet lack
the financial stability necessary for sustained economic security.
Highly concentrated consumption and limited
upward mobility
The structure of Indian household consumption reveals significant

disparities between urban and rural areas. In 2024, average month -
ly consumption per capita amounted to 6,996 rupees in urban areas
1 These authors define the middle class as individuals for whom the risk of falling back into poverty is limited (less than 4% over four years), corresponding to daily per capita income
ranging from 17 to 98 2021 PPP dollars (with vulnerability thresholds at 6.85 and 14 2017 PPP dollars, according to the World Bank). In the absence of reliable national income

data collection mechanisms in India, our study applies these thresholds to the World Bank’s consumer expenditure data, an approach aligned with the objective of assessing actual
purchasing capacity deployed in the market (although this tends to slightly underestimate the middle class in terms of income, as household savings remain unaccounted for in
expenditure measurements).
(approximately USD 84.5), compared with 4,122 rupees in rural areas
(less than USD 50). The wealthiest 5% of urban consumers spent an

average equivalent of USD 245 per month per person. Consequently,
discretionary spending power remained concentrated among the hi -
ghest income brackets.
The structure of consumption has changed over the past decade. The
proportion allocated to food purchases (34.8% of total spending on ave-
rage) declined by 4.3 percentage points between 2011–12 and 2023–
24, while allocations to transportation and communications increased
to 12.4% (+3.8 pp). Although the allocation to durable goods has in -

creased, it remains below 7% of total household spending. This increase
was driven more by a rise in the use of credit than by income growth.
Over the past ten years, consumer loans have been the main source of
household debt. Although still modest relative to GDP, household debt
rose by 12.2 pp to reach 47.8% of GDP by the end of 2025, according to
the Bank for International Settlements (BIS).

THE LABOUR MARKET SUFFERS FROM STRUCTURAL
WEAKNESSES
The limited size of the middle class and the low level of household
consumption in India can be attributed, in particular, to the enduring
structural weaknesses of the labour market. The labour market faces
two major problems: insufficient job creation and the fact that most of
the jobs created are low-skilled and low-paid.

Insufficient job creation
Despite the Indian economy’s robust growth, its employment rate re -
mains substantially lower than in comparable Asian economies: 52.7%
in 2025 according to the International Labour Organization (ILO), com-
pared with 65.6% in Indonesia and 71.3% in Vietnam. This disparity
is mainly due to low labour force participation among women (34%
compared with 53.4% and 68%) and young people (40.8% of those aged
20-24 compared with 66.7% and 69.4%).

Over the past ten years, employment growth has averaged just 2.4%
annually, whereas GDP growth was close to 6% per year.
The services sector (representing 33.2% of employment in 2025) ac -
counted for the bulk of job creation during this period (+3.2% per year),
far ahead of the manufacturing sector (+1.4%) and, above all, agricultu-
re (+0.1%), which nevertheless remains the country’s largest employer
(41.5% of the workforce). This is not enough to absorb the 10 to 12 mil-

lion new entrants to the labour market each year.
2 Employment elasticity remained relatively stable between 1990 and 2012.
A limited number of sectors have significant po-
tential for creating high-quality, well-paid jobs
To identify sectors with high potential for job creation, we calculate
employment elasticity both for aggregate economy (KLEMS data) and
for the formal manufacturing sector alone (Annual Survey of Industries,
2025).
Over the period 1981–2023, aggregate employment elasticity remained

low at a virtually unchanged level, close to 0.3. This stability, however,
masks significant sectoral divergences: elasticity remained stable in
the services sector throughout the period (0.5), but it declined shar -
ply in the manufacturing sector (from 0.4 during 1981–1990 to just
0.1 during 2012–2023 2): at the same growth rate, India now creates
far fewer industrial jobs per unit of growth than it did forty years ago
(Table 2). In the formal manufacturing sector alone, employment elas-

ticity reached 0.7 over the past ten years, driven by very robust job
creation. However, this positive trend has not been sufficient to offset
the decline in informal sector employment, which accounts for 73.4%
of total manufacturing employment but only 38% of the sector’s value
added. Labour productivity in the formal manufacturing sector is thus
estimated to be 5.4 times higher than in the informal sector.
In capital-intensive sectors, employment elasticity ranged from 0.5 to

0.6 (including electrical equipment, transportation, chemicals and me-
tals), whereas in labour-intensive sectors — specifically wood, textiles
and apparel, and manufacturing food — employment elasticities re -
mained particularly low, or in certain cases, negative.

world
In other words, growth in value added in these sectors with high labour 
absorption potential is primarily driven by productivity gains rather 
than job creation. Furthermore, even among companies operating in 
the formal sector, employment elasticity remains low at an average of 
0.4. Consequently, the traditional mechanism facilitating labour mobi-
lity from the agricultural sector to the labour-intensive manufacturing 
sector has been very limited over the past forty years.
The only manufacturing subsectors that combine strong growth, high 
employment elasticity, and high productivity — thereby offering real 
potential for creating high-quality jobs — are electronics and infor -
mation technology, electrical equipment, the automotive industry, the 
chemical industry, and, to a lesser extent, the pharmaceutical industry. 
In these subsectors, the formal employment elasticity ranges from 0.8 
to 1.2 (Chart 2). However, in 2025, these jobs accounted for just 1.4% 
of total employment and 5.9% of GDP. The potential therefore remains 
negligible at the macroeconomic level.
Several structural constraints limit job creation in 
India
- The high rigidity of the labour market: it consists mainly of small, in-
formal businesses — those with fewer than 50 employees account for 
65% of the productive sector — largely due to the stringency of labour 
legislation (for example, companies with more than 300 employees 
cannot carry out mass layoffs without authorisation).
- Numerous obstacles impede the growth of industrial and job-crea -
ting businesses: an unfavourable business environment (particularly 
difficulties in accessing land acquisition to build factories); high costs 
of accessing capital (particularly for small and medium-sized enter -
prises); insufficient export competitiveness in labour-intensive sectors 
(textiles, clothing), which encounter disadvantages, including less ad -
vantageous tariff conditions3 than competing Asian economies.
- The mismatch between the skills of the available workforce and the 
needs of businesses. This mismatch constrains wage growth and the 
development of household purchasing power, obstructs industrial ad -
vancement, and, consequently, the creation of high-quality jobs.
INADEQUATE JOB QUALITY DUE TO A LACK OF  
APPROPRIATE SKILLS
A predominantly informal and low-paid labour  
market
According to the ILO, 87.2% of total employment in India is informal, 
with 73.4% concentrated in the manufacturing sector alone, predomi -
nantly engaged in low-value-added activities that require few skills. 
Furthermore, the Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) 2023/24 indi -
cates that 58.4% of Indian workers are self-employed, 19.8% are day 
labourers, and only 21.7% are regular employees — the highest-paying 
status, with a wage gap of more than 60% compared to the other two 
categories. According to the ILO, 20% of the population captures 79% of 
aggregate labour income. The agricultural sector, which still employs 
41.5% of the labour force while contributing less than 15% to GDP, is

3 Historically, tariff rates imposed on Indian products exported to Europe have been higher than those applied to certain other Asian countries (Bangladesh, Vietnam, Pakistan).
4 According to the World Bank and the NCAER, educational and training programmes are too theoretical and fail to develop cognitive and interpersonal skills. Vocational education 
and training are not designed to meet the needs of high-demand sectors. Collaborative relationships between educational institutions and employers — particularly regarding 
internships and on-the-job training — remain inadequately developed.
3.7 times less productive than the services sector, with a median hourly 
wage that is 40 to 50% lower.
A widespread skills shortage
This concentration of earnings reflects the low skill levels of the jobs 
held: according to the PLFS, 88.2% of workers hold jobs requiring little 
or no skill. The proportion of skilled jobs is just 11.2% and has declined 
by 4.6 pp since 2018. The underlying challenge is not a shortage of 
graduates but rather a misalignment between what the labour market 
needs and the educational preparation provided to graduates 4, which 
results in their gradual downward mobility into positions far below 
their formal qualifications.
Unemployment, the downward mobility of graduates, and a shortage 
of technical skills
This disparity between skills and business needs directly contributes to 
unemployment among graduates, which reached 13.5% in 2025 accor-
ding to the ILO, compared with 1.3% for individuals without a degree. 
Within the 25- to 34-year-old demographic, the unemployment rate 
stands at 15.2% for graduates (19.2% for those with a master’s de -
gree), compared with an average of 6.1% across this age group. Only 
8.3% of graduates secure positions that genuinely correspond to their 
educational attainment; 53% of bachelor’s degree holders and 36% of 
master’s degree holders are overqualified for their positions.
In low-skilled jobs, the lack of technical training represents a signifi -
cant barrier to youth employment: according to the World Bank’s En -
terprise Survey (2022), 17.8% of traditional service companies and 14% 
of “intermediate” manufacturing companies cite this as a major obsta-
cle. In India’s manufacturing sector, 32.9% of positions are reportedly 
held by underqualified workers, according to the ILO, compared with 
28.8% in Indonesia and just 16.5% in Vietnam.
A direct impediment to industrial advancement
A survey conducted by the Ministry of Skill Development & Entrepre -
neurship across seven key sectors — automotive, textiles, agriculture, 
livestock, IT, commerce and green energy — reveals that the pace of

workforce training development significantly lags behind industry re -

are actually immediately employable.

This disparity in skills particularly constrains the transition towards
higher-value-added manufacturing activities within the sectors iden -
tified above (automotive, electronics and chemicals). Their growth
trajectory depends on technological advancement — electric vehicles,
semiconductors, specialty chemicals — and therefore on intermediate
to advanced technical skills that are currently lacking. Should trai -
ning provision fail to align with industry demand, employment elasti -

city and growth in these sectors will stagnate or decline, undermining
the only source of high-quality job creation currently available — and
consequently, the Indian economy’s ability to expand its middle class.
These structural limitations affecting national industrial development
diminish its appeal to foreign investors, despite its size. The stock of
foreign investment in India stood at USD 560 billion in Q2 2026, repre-
senting just 15% of GDP (compared with 20% in China, more than 52%

in Vietnam, and around 67% of GDP in Thailand and Malaysia).
ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE: A NEW RISK TO JOB CREATION
Artificial intelligence (AI) may also limit the expansion of high-paid
employment opportunities in the service sector. Its deployment poses
a significant threat to employment in India, particularly in the hi -
ghest-paying service sectors — the same sectors that possessed si -
gnificant potential for expanding India’s middle-class consumer base.

Agriculture (41.5% of the labour force), construction (12%), and trade/
transportation (18%) remain largely immune to the direct replacement
of workers by AI, which reduces the overall macroeconomic scale of
the coming shock. The service sectors offering the most lucrative em -
ployment — IT, finance and insurance — account for just 1.3% and 1.2%
of total employment, yet these remain the primary targets of automa-
tion. In IT services companies, the adoption of AI is expected to reduce

the need for entry-level roles (quality assurance, software testing and
support roles) but increase demand for positions centred on platform
development and management oversight. Current sector-specific evi -
dence indicates a notable slowdown in job creation within IT services
but no widespread contraction. A study by the Indian Council for Re -
search on International Economic Relations (ICRIER) on the IT sector
confirms a decline in job creation for routine and entry-level roles.

While new professions are emerging such as data centre operators, AI
DevOps engineers who serve as intermediaries between software deve-
lopment and systems operations teams, and ethical AI specialists, the
volume of employment opportunities generated remains substantially
lower than the number of jobs at risk. Furthermore, the skill require -
ments for these newly created positions exceed those possessed by the
existing cohort of graduates.
REFORMS ARE A STEP IN THE RIGHT DIRECTION BUT DO

NOT GO FAR ENOUGH
Since 2019, the Modi government has implemented a series of mea -
sures to develop industry, attract foreign direct investment, and gain
greater integration in global trade (after decades of protectionist po -
licies).
Corporate subsidy programmes (Production-Linked Incentives) have
helped support the development of those sectors with the greatest
potential for job creation (electrical, electronics, chemicals, and auto -
motive). Similarly, labour market reform (which came into force in late

2025) may alleviate impediments to business expansion and the crea-
tion of formal jobs. However, its actual impact will depend on its adop-
tion by the states, which retain considerable discretionary autonomy
over labour law, and on its effective implementation on the ground.
The free trade agreement signed between India and the European
Union in late January 2026 (which is expected to take effect in 2027 at
the earliest) should mitigate the tariff disadvantage experienced by la-

bour-intensive industries: the EU has pledged to remove tariffs, which
currently range from 4% to 12% on textiles and clothing.
However, these measures alone will be inadequate for creating
high-quality jobs if the education system fails to meet the needs of
Indian businesses. It is therefore imperative that India continues to
improve its business environment and, even more importantly, reform
its education system to promote job creation in high-value-added and

well-paying sectors. Without concrete measures, the “demographic
window” — which is expected to remain favourable for another 15 to
25 years — could close before India establishes a robust industrial
foundation and a substantial middle class.

EcoInsight 20 / 26 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
*****
While India’s economic growth has demonstrated considerable dy -
namism, this growth has not translated into the development of the
middle class. The real medium-term concern involves the emergence
of a “neo-precarious” middle class — substantial in terms of num -
bers but lacking the foundations necessary for advancing towards a
high-income economy (households with stable employment, the ability

to save, and social protection). The creation of formal, high-quality jobs
is currently inadequate to sustain industrial expansion and its progres-
sion towards higher-value segments. The cultivation of certain niche
activities, while promising, remains very limited and is far from benefi-
ting the entire population. Finally, the development of AI poses more of
a risk than a genuine opportunity for the country’s development. India
lacks both the infrastructure and the educational system necessary to

deploy AI in a way that would yield tangible benefits for its economy
and labour market.
Article completed on 19 October 2026

► 美國9月CPI年增率上升至3.7%，核心CPI年
增率由2.4%升至2.6%，核心月增率0.3%，
高於預期值0.2%。能源是通膨反彈的主因，
貢獻整體CPI月增幅超過一半；能源衝擊雖
為主導，但消費韌性使企業保有成本轉嫁能
力，住房與部分服務價格維持黏性，需求面
通膨略有提高，加上企業資本支出提升經濟
與企業獲利韌性，使總需求不易快速降溫。
► 凱投預期Fed 12月將升息1碼，明年3月再升
1碼，政策利率可能在4%以上維持更久。受
到油價高盤、通膨僵固，與財政惡化、政府
發債籌資逢企業債搶錢等影響，美歐日主要
國家公 債利 率近 期 來到高 位 。其中， 法國
2027年財政預算欲刪減支出(包括養老金、公
部門工資等)引發民眾反彈，衝擊主權信用評
等，法國CDS與法德10年期公債利差擴大、
歐元走弱，美元轉強，商品、黃金承壓。法
國政治不確定性讓歐股修正、法股最疲，成
熟國家公債殖利率升高，增加企業借貸成本
與股市評價壓力，續為金融市場擾動因子。

► 美國第三季財報季展開，市場將觀察企業在
高利率環境下的獲利表現。根據FactSet數據
顯示，標普500指數第三季獲利成長預估達
29.5%，顯示企業基本面仍具支撐，其中科
技 產業 受 惠AI需求帶動， 獲 利 成 長預 估達
65%，持續扮演企業獲利的重要成長動能。
► 聚焦科技類股，AI資本支出仍為本輪企業獲
利成長的主要驅動力。高盛指出，第三季AI

基礎建設相關企業將貢獻標普500獲利增長
的54%。此外，大型CSP之雲端業務營收有
望加速成長，反映AI運算及雲端服務需求持
續提升，有助支撐後續資本支出擴張動能。
► 近期市場波動仍受高利率環境影響，壓抑投
資人風險偏好與估值擴張空間，顯示市場目
前壓力更多來自評價面而非基本面。隨財報
數據陸續公布，若企業持續繳出優於預期的
獲利表現，並維持正向財測，市場有望逐步
回歸基本面評價，使企業獲利成長逐步支撐
股市表現。
美企財報獲利穩健，對股市中長期有撐

美股第三季獲利成長持續上修，科技為主要支撐 CSP雲端營收加速，資本支出變現跡象持續受驗證
評價面疑慮下，股價難以進一步擴張

投資焦點
► 醫療保健、醫療服務與生物技術等行業，在
景氣不好時，市場預期民眾對醫療保健產業
的需求 不易 下降 ， 成為傳 統防禦 型類 股代
表。目前金融環境利率偏高，11月美國總統
大選國會恐變天，股市波動風險上升，觀察
過去當VIX恐慌指數上揚時，醫療保健產業
相較於科技股更穩健，具有更好抗震能力。
► 醫療與生技類股近一年漲幅明顯，主因1)生
技股過去一段時間受到高利率、融資成本及
臨床試驗風險壓抑，部分公司估值偏低。當
臨床數據改善、或導入AI篩選資料與藥物，
投資人重新評價未來現金流與價值。2)大型
藥廠面臨既有藥品專利到期壓力，透過收購
取得新藥與研發管線，提高有潛力的生技公
司併購價值，帶動整體產業估值回升。3)肥
胖、癌症與慢性病用藥創造長期市場需求，
創新藥物(如瘦瘦針)讓部分醫療公司成長不
再只依賴人口老化，高盛今年7月將全球減肥
藥市場2030年銷售額預估上調15%至1,140
億美元，看好此類藥品的長期商業潛力。
醫療生技近年表現佳，估今年3Q醫院客流量年增4%VIX恐慌指數上升時，健護醫療表現相較穩健

市場波動上升、醫療保健表現佳，獲利穩定成長

### Page 8

8
內部參考文件請勿外流
► 美國醫療費用深受法令影響，由於商業雇主
贊助保險(ESI)和平價醫療法案(ACA)的自付
額增加，以及最高自付費用要求，較去年同
期提高，使今年第三季美國整體住院人數、
手術量和醫院客流量均呈現改善。高盛預估
今年第三季醫院客流量年增4%，加上季節性
因素，下半年醫療保健利用率將較上半年成
長，整體第四季預估獲利穩健，甚至可延至
明年，反應在近期XLV ETF股價創新高。
► 展望2027年，各醫療保險計畫普遍優先考慮
透過提高會員保費，和要求費用分攤(尤其是
提高會員自付費用上限)來擴大利潤空間，並
持續優化覆蓋範圍和產品組合，預估商業雇
主提供的醫療保險(ESI)保費趨勢，將在明年
加速成長至過去20 年來的最高水準。 以
Refinitiv預估明年美國各產業獲利成長來看，
醫療保健和生技相較於今年獲利明顯增加，
反而今年獲利極佳的能源和通訊受高基期影
響，獲利增速下滑。投資人在整體金融環境
波動放大時期，宜以醫療生技股穩中求利。

股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長，市場靜待財報結果與展望，和對AI資本支出回收影響。
考量地緣衝突、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉，短期金融市場紛擾不止，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變，逢股市拉回時可分批布局，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點，全球利率走揚有利銀行股。此外，地緣衝突頻傳，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間，殖利率偏高環境使短中期債券提供吸
引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利
差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等

匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。
 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。
美元高檔有撐
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期難解，油價維持高位但震盪加劇。
 短期美國雙率走高，使金價短期承壓，惟美歐日財政赤字壓力與去美元化，使全球央行與長期投資
人買盤依舊，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書(或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用(境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金(不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施)；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。
